\mathtt{p}_{c},\mathtt{p}_{k}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{S})
.

Remark
.

In fact, the converse of
3.17
is also true: if there exists no
\mathtt{p}_{c}
satisfying cases 1 or 2, then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
.
We will explain the proof in the remarks after
3.18
and
3.25
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof we omit
\psi^{*}
on the left (as usual
\psi^{*}
denotes everything that
\psi_{k}
commutes through on the left).
We also write

v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}=\chi_{h+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h+1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h+2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h+2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{d}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{d}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{d}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}.

Case 1

a)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
,
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
appears at the end of the arm:

We see that if
\mathtt{T}
satisfies the conditions of the statement, then it has corresponding reduced expression

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h-1}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda},\\

where the indices
k+2,k+6,\dots,y_{h-1},y_{h}
must be four apart by the minimality of
c
.
Hence we have that
y_{h-1}=k+2+4(h-2)
and
y_{h}=k+2+4(h-1)=c-4
.
It is also clear that the first entry that
\psi_{k}
does not commute with is
\Psi_{k+2}
as
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
are in the leg and
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{k}=0
.
We also know that
\chi_{i}
and
\chi_{i+1}
commute if the corresponding
\Psi_{y_{i}}
commute, that is
y_{i}\leq y_{i+1}-4
.
Further,
\chi_{1}
is always nonempty.
Then by
3.12
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\underbrace{\chi_{2}\chi_{1}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}}_{\text{as $\chi_{1}$ and $\chi_{2}$ commute and $\chi_{1}(\mathfrak{l})=\psi_{k+4}$}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}+1}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-8}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}.

We must have
\chi_{h}(\mathfrak{l})=\psi_{c-2}
, otherwise we cannot have
\mathtt{p}_{c}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
We will write
\chi_{h}=\widehat{\chi}_{h}\chi_{h}(\mathfrak{l})
.
Then

\displaystyle\chi_{h}(\mathfrak{l})\chi_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-1})\psi_{c-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}=0

\displaystyle+(y_{c-2})\psi_{c-3}\psi_{c-4}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle-2(y_{c-1})\psi_{c-3}\psi_{c-4}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}=0

\displaystyle+(y_{c})\psi_{c-3}\psi_{c-4}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}=0

\displaystyle=\psi_{c-4}\psi_{c-5}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda},

hence

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-8}}{\downarrow}}\widehat{\chi}_{h}\psi_{c-4}\psi_{c-5}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

as required.

b)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0),\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
is not at the end of the arm:

We have that

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h-3}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h-1}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}},

where
k+2,k+6,\dots,y_{h-1}
are all four apart.
We also have that
y_{h-1}=k+2+4(h-2)=c-4
and
y_{h}=c-2
, thus
\chi_{h-1}
and
\chi_{h}
do not commute.
Then as before, we have that

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-4)}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

Due to the conditions on
\mathtt{T}
we must have that
\chi_{h}=\psi_{c-1}(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{c+1})\psi_{c}
where
x\geq c
, otherwise
\mathtt{p}_{c}
cannot be in the arm.
Hence

\displaystyle\chi_{h-1}(\mathfrak{l})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{c-2}\psi_{c-1}(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{c+1})\psi_{c}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{c+1})\psi_{c}\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

by
3.15
, so

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\widehat{\chi}_{h-1}(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{c+1})\psi_{c}\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0),\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)
:

Here we have that

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

Once again, the indices
k+2,k+6,\dots,c-6=k+2+4(h-2),c-2
are all four apart and we also see that
\chi_{h}=1
, hence

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-1)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

The second part of the proof considers the three cases when
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, in which case
\psi_{k}
does not commute with
\chi_{1}
.

d)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1),\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
appears at the end of the arm:

Here

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda},

where the chain on the left might contain the additional terms
\psi_{k-3}
and
\psi_{k+3}
depending on whether
k-2
and
k+4
are in the arm.
In the proof, we will omit writing these as they do not play an active part in the rotation.
It is clear that
y_{1}=k-4
and
y_{2}=k+4
as both
\mathtt{p}_{k}
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
are in the leg.
After these two
\Psi
-chains, just as before,
k+8,\dots,k+2+4(i-1),\dots,y_{h-1},y_{h}
are all
4
apart.
Observe that
y_{h}\neq c-4
can only happen if
c=k+4
, in which case
h=1
and
v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}
.
We will deal with this special case at the end of the proof, and for now we will assume that
y_{h}=c-4
.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}(\psi_{k}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k})\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}(\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\underbrace{\psi_{k+1})\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}}_{=0\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{even}}}

\displaystyle+\psi_{k+2}(y_{k})\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-2\psi_{k+2}(y_{k+1})\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}\\
\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}\\
+\psi_{k+2}(y_{k+2})\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}\\
\hskip 85.35826pt\cdot(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}\end{cases}=0\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{standard-y}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}(\chi_{2}\Psi_{y_{3}}\Psi_{y_{3}-2})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-8}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-8}}{\downarrow}}\widehat{\chi}_{h}\psi_{c-4}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

where the product
\chi_{h}\chi_{\ell}
gives the same result as in case a).

Finally, suppose that
y_{h}<c-4
and
c=k+4
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}(\psi_{k}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k})\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}(\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\underbrace{\psi_{k+1})\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}}_{=0\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{even}}}

\displaystyle+\psi_{k+2}(y_{k})\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}-2\psi_{k+2}(y_{k+1})\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}\\
+\psi_{k+2}(y_{k+2})\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}\end{cases}=0\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{standard-y}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{\ell}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+3}\psi_{k+2})\psi_{k+1}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots\qquad\text{ see the computation for $\chi_{h}(\mathfrak{l})\chi_{\ell}$ above}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{h}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}.

e)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
,
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
is not at the end of the arm:

Similar to case b).
Here, the chain ends with
\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}
where
k+2+4(h-2)\leq c-4
.
Further, because of the shifted residue of
\mathtt{p}_{k}
, we have that
y_{1}\leq k-4
.
If
k+2+4(h-2)=c-4
,
\chi_{h-1}
and
\chi_{h}
do not commute:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{1}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\;\,\vdots\text{ see the computation in case $d)$}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{1}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{1}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})

\displaystyle\cdot\widehat{\chi}_{h-1}(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{c+1})\psi_{c}\psi_{c-1}\psi_{c-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-4}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

If
k+2+4(h-2)<c-4
, we have that
v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\dots\psi_{x+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{x}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}
where
x<k-4
, but the proof is essentially the same.

f)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1),\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)
:

As in case c),
\chi_{h}=\operatorname{id}
and
\psi_{k}\chi_{1}
behaves the same way as in the previous case.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\dots\psi_{y_{1}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-2)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{1}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2+4(h-3)}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

Case 2

We have the same six subcases as in Case
1
and with some slight modifications these work the same way as before, however
\psi_{k}
will start acting on the end of the chain (that is the highest indexed
\Psi
-term) and we will be using
3.12
part
3
and
3.14
(instead of
3.12
part
2
and
3.15
).
Details can be included by switching the toggle in the
arXiv
version of this paper.
∎

3.4.
At least three consecutive pairs in
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
or
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})

Now that we have shown what happens when we act with
\psi_{k}
when
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
are in
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
is in
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
(or vice versa,
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
), we will extend with these results for at least three pairs in the leg (or in the arm) of
\mathtt{T}
.

Lemma 3.18

(cf.
[
Spe15
]
, Lemma 3.19)
.

Let
\mathtt{T}\in\operatorname{Std}(\lambda)
with
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
.

(1)

If
a
is odd, and

a)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
m_{1}\leq k\leq y_{1}
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h
either
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
or
y_{i}=4i+k-2
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
;

b)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
m_{1}\leq k+2\leq y_{1}
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h
either
y_{i}\geq 4i+k+2
or
y_{i}=4i+k
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
.

(2)

If
a
is even, and

c)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a-1
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
m_{1}\leq k\leq y_{1}
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h-1
either
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
or
y_{i}=4i+k-2
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
y_{h}\geq 4h+k
or
y_{h}=4h+k-2,\chi_{h}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell>4h+k+2
;

d)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a-1
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
m_{1}\leq k+2\leq y_{1}
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h-1
either
y_{i}\geq 4i+k+2
or
y_{i}=4i+k
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
y_{h}\geq 4h+k+2
or
y_{h}=4h+k,\chi_{h}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell>4h+k+4
.

Proof.

Just like in the proof of
3.17
, we omit writing
\psi^{*}
on the left.
We will also write
\chi_{i}=\widehat{\chi}_{i}\chi_{i}(\mathfrak{l})
(recall that
\chi_{i}(\mathfrak{l})=\psi_{y_{i}+2}
).
We prove the statement by induction on the length of
v_{\mathtt{T}}
.
The proof itself is very similar to the proof of
3.17
.

“If” part:

(1)

Odd
a

As
\mathtt{p}_{k}
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
are both in the leg, we immediately see that
y_{1}\geq k+2
.
In addition, we only care about
\chi_{1}
if
y_{1}=k+2
because in all other cases the chain is long enough to propagate the leftmost
\psi_{k+4}
in
\Psi_{k+4}
from
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}
, instead of
\psi_{k+4}
from
\chi_{1}
.
Now we can break down the proof into several subcases:

a)

Assume that
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
and
y_{1}\geq k+4
.

If
h=1
, we have that

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=0.

Now suppose that
v_{\mathtt{T}}=\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}
where
h>1
as stated in the lemma.
Then from the base case we have that

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\chi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\underbrace{\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}.

As
m_{1}\leq k
, it implies that
m_{2}\leq k+2
and by assumption, we also have that
k+6\leq y_{2}
.

\circ

If
y_{2}\geq k+8
we have that

\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}=\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+10}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}},

where the first and last chains might be empty, but our conditions guarantee that
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}
appears.
This forces
\mathtt{p}_{k+4},\mathtt{p}_{k+6}
to be in the leg of
\mathtt{T}^{\prime}
.
Thus

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+10}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}=0

by induction, since for each
i=1,2,\dots,h-1
, we have that either
y_{i+1}\geq 4i+(k+4)
or
y_{i+1}\geq 4i+(k+2)
(these follow immediately from the conditions on
v_{\mathtt{T}}
).

\circ

If
y_{2}=k+6
we have that

\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}=\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}

and we also see that
\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
.
Once again we see that
\mathtt{p}_{k+4}
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+6}
are in the leg of
\mathtt{T}^{\prime}
, hence

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}=0

by induction for the same reasoning as in the previous case.

Similarly, we also need to consider the case when
y_{1}=k+2
.
By assumption, we must have that
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
.

If
h=1
, we have that

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\widehat{\chi}_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}z^{\lambda}=0,

where
\chi_{1}=\widehat{\chi}_{1}\psi_{k+4}
.

Now suppose that
h>1
and
v_{\mathtt{T}}=\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}
is as in the statement.
From the base case we have that

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\widehat{\chi}_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}\underbrace{\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}.

By assumption
y_{2}\geq k+6
, and this forces
\mathtt{p}_{k+4}
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+6}
into
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T}^{\prime})
.
We also know that
\psi_{k+4}
and
\chi_{2}
commute.
Then we have two options:

\circ

if
y_{2}\geq k+8
, we have that

\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}=\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+10}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}

and then

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\widehat{\chi}_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}=0

by induction (see the proof of
y_{1}\geq k+4
); or

\circ

if
y_{2}=k+6
, we have that

\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}=\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}

and then

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\widehat{\chi}_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}=0

by induction (works entirely the same as the previous cases).

b)

Assume that
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
.

In this case, we will have an additional chain on the left of
\Psi_{y_{1}}
such that
\psi_{k}
appears in
\chi_{0}
: as
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
are in the leg in
(0,1)
-positions, we have some
r\leq k-2
in
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
in the position
\mathtt{T}(1,m_{0}-1)
and some
s\geq k+2
in the position
\mathtt{T}(1,m_{0})
.
Hence the chain
\chi_{0}
which moves
s
into the right position must involve the terms
\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}
in this order.
Notice that
\psi_{k}
acts on these additional terms exactly the same way as described in cases d)-f) in
3.17
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{s-1}\dots\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{0}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{s-1}\dots\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{0}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{s-1}\dots\psi_{k+3}\psi_{k-2}\dots\psi_{y_{0}+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\chi_{1}\underbrace{\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=0\text{ by case a).}

(The chain
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}
might be empty though.)

Now we see why the statement requires different lower bounds for
y_{i}
if
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
.
Even though we would like to determine the action of
\psi_{k}
on
v_{\mathtt{T}}
, in fact we are looking at
\psi_{k+2}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}
(as
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
with shifted residues it follows that
y_{1}\geq k+2
).
Applying case a) to this expression and shifting the indices appropriately completes the proof.

(2)

Even
a

c)

Suppose that
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
and
y_{1}\geq k+2
.
If
h=1
and
y_{1}\geq k+4
, we have that

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\underbrace{(\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{k+8})\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+6}\psi_{k+5}\psi_{k+4}\dots\psi_{a}}_{=\chi_{d}}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots\,\;\text{for the computation see \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{3pairs} Case $1a)$}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}\psi_{k+3}\psi_{k+5}(\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{k+8})\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+2}\dots\psi_{a}\psi_{k+6}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=0.

Note that
\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{k+8}
might be empty, but our conditions guarantee that
\psi_{k+6}
is there to eliminate
z^{\lambda}
.

A similar reasoning as in the previous cases shows that if
h>1
, then for all
i=1,2,\dots,h-2
we either have that
y_{i+1}\geq 4i+(k+4)
or
y_{i+1}\geq 4i+(k+2)
if
\chi_{i+1}\neq 1
.
Then, unless
y_{h}=4h+k-2
, it immediately follows that
\ell>4h+k
and if
y_{h}=4h+k-2
, we know that
\ell>y_{h}+4
by assumption.
We see that on the right end of the chain we will have an additional braid relation (for shorthand notation, let
s:=4h+k
), where
\psi_{s}
comes from either
\chi_{h}
(if
y_{h}\neq s
) or from
\Psi_{s}
(if
y_{h}=s
).
Hence, in order to complete the proof, it suffices to consider the following:

\displaystyle\psi_{s}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{s}\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{s+2}\psi_{s+1}\psi_{s}\psi_{s-1}\psi_{s-2}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{s+2}(\psi_{s}\psi_{s+1}\psi_{s})\psi_{s-1}\psi_{s-2}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{s+3}\psi_{s+2}\psi_{s-2}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{s+3}\psi_{s-2}\dots\psi_{a}\psi_{s+2}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=0.

d)

Take
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
.

The proof is a combination of the proofs of cases b) and c).

Converse:

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, let
t
be minimal such that
y_{t}\leq 4t+k-4
or
y_{t}=4t+k-2
and
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
(or if
a
is even and
t=h
, then let
y_{t}=4t+k-2,\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
or
\ell=4t+k+2
).
We see that if such a
t
exists, it means that there is at least one more pair
\mathtt{p}_{c}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
such that
k+2<c
.
Indeed, if
y_{t}=4t+k-2
and
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
, it is easy to see that
\mathtt{p}_{4t+k}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
If
y_{t}\leq 4t+k-4
, by minimality of
t
we know that either
y_{t-1}\geq 4t+k-4
or
y_{t-1}=4t+k-6
with
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
.
If
y_{t-1}\geq 4t+k-4
then
y_{t-1}\nless y_{t}
.
Hence,
y_{t-1}=4t+k-6
and
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
.
But this immediately implies that
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id}
too, thus
\mathtt{p}_{4t+k-2}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
For
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, we can repeat the previous argument with increasing all indices by
2
.

If
\mathtt{p}_{c}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
and there are no other pairs between
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
and
\mathtt{p}_{c}
, we have already shown in
3.17
that
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}\neq 0
.
Hence, from now on we will assume that there exists some minimal
c
such that
k+2<c<d
where
\mathtt{p}_{c}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
and
\mathtt{p}_{d}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.

By assumption,
y_{1}\geq k
, but as
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
, we have that
y_{1}\geq k+2
.
If
y_{1}=k+2
and
\chi_{1}=\operatorname{id}
, then we would have that
\mathtt{p}_{k+4}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
Thus we can assume that
t\geq 2
.

If
t=2
and
\mathtt{p}_{k}=(1,0)
, by minimality of
t
we have the following:

•

y_{1}=k+2
with
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
or
y_{1}\geq k+4
, and

•

y_{2}\leq k+4
or
y_{2}=k+6
and
\chi_{2}=\operatorname{id}
or
y_{2}=k+6,\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell=k+10
.

As
y_{1}<y_{2}
, we see that
k+2\leq y_{1}\leq k+4
.

If
t=2
and
\mathtt{p}_{k}=(0,1)
, we must have that:

•

y_{1}=k+4
with
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
or
y_{1}\geq k+6
,

•

y_{2}\leq k+6
or
y_{2}=k+8
and
\chi_{2}=\operatorname{id}
or
y_{2}=k+8,\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell=k+12
.

As
y_{1}<y_{2}
, we see that
k+4\leq y_{1}\leq k+6
.

Hence, for
t=2
we need to consider only the following cases:

(1)

\bf{\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)}

a)

y_{1}=k+4,y_{2}=k+6
and
\chi_{2}=\operatorname{id}
, which forces
\chi_{1}=\operatorname{id}
(
a
can be even or odd):

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\underbrace{\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{d})z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

The next two sets of conditions deal with the special case when
a
is even,
t=2=h
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+10}
appears at the end of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
These immediately force
y_{2}=k+6,\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell=k+10
.

b)

y_{1}=k+2
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\chi_{1}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\underbrace{\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+8}}_{\chi_{2}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+6}\psi_{k+5}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

c)

y_{1}=k+4
. Here
\chi_{1}=\operatorname{id}
or
\chi_{1}=(\psi_{k+5})\psi_{k+6}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\Psi_{k+4}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\underbrace{(\psi_{k+7})\psi_{k+8}}_{\chi_{2}}\psi_{k+9}\psi_{k+8}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{k+7})\psi_{k+6}\psi_{k+5}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}.

(2)

\bf{\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)}

Recall from the previous part that
\chi_{0}
contains the subchain
\psi_{k+2}\psi_{k+1}\dots\psi_{k-2}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that the leftmost entry in
\chi_{0}
is precisely
\psi_{k+2}
as
\psi
terms with larger indices can be absorbed into
\psi^{*}
on the left.
For shorthand notation we also write
\chi^{\prime}_{0}=\psi_{k-2}\psi_{k-3}\dots\psi_{y_{0}+2}
.
For the computation of
\psi_{k}\chi_{0}
, details can be found in part 1b) of this proof.

d)

y_{1}=k+6,y_{2}=k+8
and
\chi_{2}=\operatorname{id}
, which forces
\chi_{1}=\operatorname{id}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}\underbrace{\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+6}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

e)

y_{1}=k+4,\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id},y_{2}=k+8
and
\chi_{2}=\operatorname{id}
:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\underbrace{\psi_{k+2}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}_{\text{apply \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{3pairs}}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

f)

y_{1}=k+4,y_{2}=k+6
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}\neq\chi_{2}
(note that in this case
\chi_{1}=\psi_{k+5}\psi_{k+6},\chi_{2}=(\psi_{k+9})\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+8}
, so they do not commute with each other):

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+5}\underbrace{\psi_{k+6}\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+8}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}}_{\text{apply \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{halfdom-leg}}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\chi^{\prime}_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{0}^{\prime}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+5}\psi_{k+8}\psi_{k+7}\psi_{k+6}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

Finally, as for
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, we consider the case when
a
is even
t=2=h
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+12}
is at the end of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
separately.
Here we immediately see that
y_{2}=k+8,\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\ell=k+12
.

g)

y_{1}=k+4
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
; and

h)

y_{1}=k+6
and
\chi_{1}=\operatorname{id}
or
\chi_{1}=\psi_{k+8}
.

We omit these proofs as they are essentially the same as in the
(1,0)
-case.

Now suppose that
t>2
.

First, we look at the different combinations of the first two
\Psi
-chains on the left.
(As
t>2
, it ensures that the propagation cannot stop here.)
We have several cases to consider:

(1)

\bf{\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)}

a)

If
y_{1}=k+2,y_{2}=k+6
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}\neq\chi_{2}
, we have that:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\chi_{1}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

b)

If
y_{1}=k+2,y_{2}\geq k+8
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
(note that
\chi_{2}
might be empty), then:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\widehat{\chi}_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

c)

If
y_{1}=k+4,y_{2}=k+6
and
\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
(note that
\chi_{1}
might be empty), we have that:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\Psi_{k+4}\psi_{k}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

d)

If
y_{1}\geq k+4,y_{2}\geq k+8
(note that both
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
might be empty), then we have that:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k}\Psi_{k+2}\Psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi_{k+6}\Psi_{k+4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{3}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{3}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

(2)

\bf{\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)}

a)

y_{1}=k+4,y_{2}=k+8
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}\neq\chi_{2}
;

b)

y_{1}=k+4,y_{2}\geq k+10
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
(
\chi_{2}
might be empty);

c)

y_{1}\geq k+6,y_{2}=k+8
and
\chi_{2}\neq\operatorname{id}
(
\chi_{1}
might be empty);

b)

y_{1}\geq k+6
and
y_{2}\geq k+10
(both
\chi_{1}
and
\chi_{2}
might be empty).

We observe that these are the same conditions as for
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, except that the indices are increased by
2
.
The computations are identical.

For
2\leq i\leq t-1
, we claim that whenever
4i+k\leq y_{i}
, then

(3.5)

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

and whenever
4i+k-2=y_{i}
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
, then

(3.6)

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{i}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

We will prove
eqs.

3.5
and
3.6
by induction on
i
.
If
i=2
and
k+4\leq y_{1}
, then

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda},

and if
y_{1}=k+2
and
\chi_{1}\neq\operatorname{id}
, then

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

For the induction step, we break down the proof into several subcases.
We have four cases to consider:

(1)

We show that if for all
2\leq i-1\leq t-2
either
eq.

3.5
or
eq.

3.6
holds and
y_{i-1}\geq 4i+k-4
and
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
, then
eq.

3.5
follows for
i
.

As
y_{i-1}\geq 4i+k-4
we can assume by induction that

(3.7)

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{i-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

As
i-1\leq t-2
and
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
we have that

\displaystyle\,\,\,\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}
