\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi_{4i+k-4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

(2)

We show that if for all
2\leq i-1\leq t-2
either
eq.

3.5
or
eq.

3.6
holds and
y_{i-1}\geq 4i+k-4,y_{i}=4i+k-2,\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
, then
eq.

3.6
follows for
i
.

As
y_{i-1}\geq 4i+k-4
we can assume that
eq.

3.7
holds.
Then:

\displaystyle\,\,\,\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi_{4i+k-4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

(3)

We show that if for all
2\leq i-1\leq t-2
either
eq.

3.5
or
eq.

3.6
holds and
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6,\chi_{i-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
then
eq.

3.5
follows for
i
.

As
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6
and
\chi_{i-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
we can assume by induction that

(3.8)

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{i-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{i-1}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

As
y_{i}\geq 4i+k
,
\chi_{i-1}
and
\chi_{i}
commute, so:

\displaystyle\,\,\,\chi_{i-1}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i}\widehat{\chi}_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{4i+k-4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i}\widehat{\chi}_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{4i+k-4}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

as
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6
.

(4)

We show that if for all
2\leq i-1\leq t-2
either
eq.

3.5
or
eq.

3.6
holds and
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6,\chi_{i-1}\neq\operatorname{id},y_{i}=4i+k-2
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
, then
eq.

3.6
follows for
i
.

As
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6
and
\chi_{i-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
we can assume that
eq.

3.8
holds.
As
y_{i}=4i+k-2
,
\chi_{i-1}
and
\chi_{i}
commute, so:

\displaystyle\,\,\,\chi_{i-1}\chi_{i}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i}\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-2}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i}\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{4i+k-6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\chi_{i-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{i}\chi_{i+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda},

as
y_{i-1}=4i+k-6
.

Finally, we will use
eqs.

3.5
and
3.6
to see what happens at the
t
-th chain.
We write
s=4t+k
.

a)

Suppose that
\chi_{t-1}=\operatorname{id}=\chi_{t}
.
By minimality of
t
, we have that
y_{t-1}\geq s-4
.
On the other hand,
s-2\geq y_{t}>y_{t-1}\geq s-4
, so in fact
y_{t-1}=s-4
and
y_{t}=s-2
.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

by
eq.

3.5

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

We see that the last line is in our standard form and is non-zero.

b)

Suppose
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}\neq\chi_{t}
.
As
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
, we have that
y_{t-1}\geq s-6
.
By minimality of
t
and using the fact that
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id}
, we also see that
y_{t}\leq s-4
or if
t=h
then
y_{t}=s-2
.
On the other hand,
s-4\geq y_{t}>y_{t-1}\geq s-6
.
Thus,
y_{t}=s-4
and
y_{t-1}=s-6
.
We also see that
\chi_{t-1}=(\psi_{s-5})\psi_{s-4}
and
\chi_{t}=(\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{s-1})\psi_{s-3}\psi_{s-2}
and they do not commute (compare with case 2f) above).
Here
x+1=\mathtt{T}(1,m_{t})
.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\chi_{t}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-2}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

by
eq.

3.6

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}^{\prime}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

using
3.15
.
Here
\chi_{t-1}^{\prime}=(\psi_{s-5}\psi_{x}\dots\psi_{s-1})\psi_{s-2}\psi_{s-3}\psi_{s-4}
.
If
t\neq h
or
\chi_{d}=\operatorname{id}
, then the second line is in our standard form and is non-zero.
Otherwise, we need to consider the special case where
t=h
,
a
is even and
\ell=s+2
.
In this case,
\chi_{t}=\psi_{s-3}\psi_{s}\psi_{s-1}\psi_{s-2}
.
Then:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\chi_{t}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

by
eq.

3.6

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{s-1}\psi_{s-2}\psi_{s-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}.

c)

Suppose
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
and
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
.
As
\chi_{t-1}\neq\operatorname{id}
, we have that
s-4\geq y_{t-1}\geq s-6
.
By minimality of
t
, we also see that
y_{t}\leq s-2
.
On the other hand,
s-6\leq y_{t-1}<y_{t}\leq s-2
.
If
y_{t}=y_{t-1}+2
, this would force
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id}
(see case b)).
Hence,
y_{t}=s-2
and
y_{t-1}=s-6
.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

by
eq.

3.6

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

We see that the final line is in our standard form and is non-zero.

d)

Suppose that
\chi_{t-1}=\operatorname{id}
and
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id}
.
Observe that this case can only happen if
a
is even with
\ell=s+2,t=h
and
y_{t}=s-2
.
By minimality of
t
, we have that
y_{t-1}\geq s-4
.
On the other hand,
s-2=y_{t}>y_{t-1}\geq s-4
, thus
y_{t-1}=s-4
.
We also see that
\chi_{t}=(\psi_{s-1})\psi_{s}
.
Using
eq.

3.5
, we have that:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\Psi_{s-4}\chi_{t}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{s-4}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{3}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{s-1})\psi_{s-2}\psi_{s-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}.

We see that the last line is in our standard form and is non-zero.

For
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, we need to consider cases a)– d) as above, but with the appropriate indices increased by
2
.
∎

Remark
.

Observe that
3.17
part 1 corresponds to the case where
t=c
and for all
1\leq i<t
we have that
y_{i}=4i+k-2
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
(or
y_{i}=4i+k
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
for
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
).
Thus, the first part of the proof of
3.18
also shows the converse of
3.17
part 1, that is if there exists no
\mathtt{p}_{c}
as given in the statement then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
.

Definition 3.19
.

Suppose that
k
is odd such that
\vphantom{1}\smash{1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{v}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{1}{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{1}}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{m}{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{m}}
\vphantom{1}\smash{\ell}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k{+}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k{+}2}
\vphantom{1}\smash{x{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{x}
\mathtt{T}=

where
v
is minimal such that
k<v
, the section of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})

v,\dots,p_{1}-1,p_{1},\dots,p_{m}=2r+k
contains
r
entries and the section of
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})

k-1,k,\dots,x
contains
r+2
entries such that there are
m
(resp.,
m+1
) pairs amongst these
r
(resp.,
r+2
) entries.
Note that if
v+1\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
then
v=p_{1}-1
.

In this case, we define
(\mathtt{T})^{2r+k,k}
to be the standard
\lambda
-tableau where
\mathtt{p}_{k}

\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T}),\mathtt{p}_{p_{m}}=\mathtt{p}_{2r+k}

\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
and all other elements of
\operatorname{Arm}((\mathtt{T})^{2r+k,k})
and
\operatorname{Leg}((\mathtt{T})^{2r+k,k})
agree with those of
\mathtt{T}
.
This can be easily visualised as a ‘clockwise’ rotation of the entries
k-1,k,\dots,2r+k
.
Thus,
\vphantom{1}\smash{1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{k}
\vphantom{1}\smash{v}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{1}{-}1}

\vphantom{1}\smash{x}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{m}{-}1}
\vphantom{1}\smash{p_{m}}
(\mathtt{T})^{p_{m},k}=

where the omitted parts are identical to those in
\mathtt{T}
.

Corollary 3.21
.

Assume that
\mathtt{T}\in\operatorname{Std}(\lambda)
and
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
.

(1)

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, let

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

Suppose
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}\neq 0
and that
t
is minimal such that
y_{t}\leq 4t+k-4
or
y_{t}=4t+k-2
and
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
or if
a
is even and
t=h
, assume that
y_{t}=4t+k-2
and either
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
or
\ell=4t+k+2
.

(2)

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, let

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\chi_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

Suppose
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}\neq 0
and that
t
is minimal such that
y_{t}\leq 4t+k-2
or
y_{t}=4t+k
and
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
or if
a
is even and
t=h
, assume that
y_{t}=4t+k
and either
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
or
\ell=4t+k+4
.

Then there exists some minimal
r
such that
\mathtt{T}
is as in
3.19
and
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}v_{(\mathtt{T})^{2r+k,k}}
which is in reduced form.

Proof.

We will continue to use the notation
s=4t+k
from the proof of
3.18
.
Observe that the final part of the proof of
3.18
has all the necessary results to prove the statement.
First, we assume that
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
.
The case
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
can be proved analogously.

a)

If
\chi_{t}=\operatorname{id}
, then this corresponds to cases a) and c) in the proof of
3.18
.
In this case,

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})(\chi_{t-1})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

We are considering the entries from
k-1
up to
y_{t}+2=s
, out of which
2t
are in the arm and
2t+2
are in the leg.

Setting
r=2t
, it is easy to see that
\mathtt{T}
is as in
3.19
.
Assume that the set
k+3,k+4,\dots,s-3,s-2
contains
2d
entries in total.
We have just shown that these entries are evenly distributed between the arm and the leg, that is there are
d
many of them in
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
.
If we start counting the entries from
k+3
up to
s-2
in the natural order, whenever an even (resp. odd) entry
g
is unpaired in
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
, then
g+1
(resp.
g-1
) is in
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
We can assume that there are
f-1
pairs between
k+3
and
s-2
in
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
Hence, there must be
f-1+2=f+1
pairs in
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
between
k-1
and
s-2
(the plus two accounts for the pairs
\mathtt{p}_{k}
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
).
Thus, we see that
\mathtt{T}
is indeed as in
3.19
where
\mathtt{p}_{s}=\mathtt{p}_{p_{m}},f=m
and
r=2t
.
Looking at the reduced expression, this is the rotation that moves
\mathtt{p}_{k}
into the arm and
\mathtt{p}_{s}
into the leg.

b)

If
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id}
and either
t\neq h
or
\chi_{d}=\operatorname{id}
, then this is case b) in the proof of
3.18
and

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi^{\prime}_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{t+1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t+1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}.

We are considering the entries from
k-1
up to
y_{t}+2=s-2
.
But
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{s-2}=1
, so in total we move
2t-1
entries in the arm and
2t+1
in the leg.
Setting
r=2t-1
and applying the arguments from the previous paragraph, we see that
\mathtt{T}
is as in
3.19
.

c)

Finally, if
\chi_{t}\neq\operatorname{id},t=h
and
\chi_{d}\neq\operatorname{id}
, then this is case d) and the special case of case b) in the proof of
3.18
.
We have that

\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi_{k}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{t-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-2}}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{t-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{t}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{t-1}}}{\downarrow}}(\psi_{s-1})\psi_{s-2}\psi_{s-3}\dots\psi_{a}z^{\lambda}.

We are considering the entries
k-1,k,\dots,s+2
.
In total, we have
4t+4
of them,
2t+1
are in the arm and
2t+3
are in the leg.
Setting
r=2t+1
and applying the arguments from case a), we see that
\mathtt{T}
is as in
3.19
.
∎

Recall from
3.7
that
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{x}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y}}{\uparrow}}:=\Psi_{x}\Psi_{x+2}\dots\Psi_{y}
.

Definition 3.22

(cf.
[
Spe15
]
, Def. 3.22)
.

Let
\mathtt{T}\in\operatorname{Std}(\lambda)
.
The corresponding
v_{\mathtt{T}}
can be written the following way, which we call reverse normal form RNF (compare with
section

3.3
).

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\rho_{0}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{d}z^{\lambda}

Here
y_{i+1}\leq y_{i}-2
and
m_{i+1}=m_{i}-2
for
1\leq i\leq h
.
If
a
is odd, then
m_{h}=a
and
\rho_{d}=\operatorname{id}
.
Otherwise (when
a
is even),
m_{h}=a-1
and
\rho_{d}=\psi_{\ell-1}\dots\psi_{a}=\chi_{d}
.

Similarly to
\chi_{i}
, unless
\rho_{i}=\operatorname{id}
, we denote the rightmost entry of
\rho_{i}
by
\rho_{i}(\mathfrak{l})=y_{i+1}-2
.
Observe that the leftmost entry in
\rho_{i+1}
is strictly less than
y_{i}-2
.
It is easy to see that
\rho_{i}=\operatorname{id}
whenever
y_{i}+4\geq y_{i-1}
.
Further,
\rho_{0}
corresponds to a chain of single
\psi
’s with increasing indices from left to right where
\rho_{0}(\mathfrak{l})=\psi_{m_{1}+2}
.
This chain appears if the pair at the end of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
has shifted residues.

Remark
.

The action of RNF can be visualised as placing entries into
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
, as opposed to the normal form where we were placing entries into
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.
To be more specific, RNF puts the pairs
\mathtt{p}_{y_{h}},\mathtt{p}_{y_{h-1}},\dots,\mathtt{p}_{y_{1}}
at the beginning of
\operatorname{Leg}(\mathtt{T})
(with some possible shifts coming from the
\rho
’s).

.

Let
n=18,\lambda=(8,1^{10})
and assume that

is as below.

\vphantom{1}\smash{12}
\vphantom{1}\smash{13}
\vphantom{1}\smash{15}
\vphantom{1}\smash{17}
\mathtt{T}=

Then

\displaystyle v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{7}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{3}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{5}}{\downarrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{5}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{9}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{15}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{7}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{13}}{\downarrow}}\psi_{17}\psi_{16}\dots\psi_{8}

\displaystyle=\psi_{15}\Psi_{13}\psi_{7}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{9}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{11}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{5}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{9}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{3}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{7}}{\uparrow}}\psi_{17}\psi_{16}\dots\psi_{8}.

Example 3.24
.

We will illustrate using RNF by going through the different cases outlined in
3.17
part 2.

a)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)
,
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
:

As
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)=\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}
, we have that
\rho_{0}=\operatorname{id}=\rho_{h}
.
Also, note that that for each
1\leq i\leq h
the chain
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i+1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i+1}}}{\downarrow}}
contains one more
\Psi
than
\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{i}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{i}}}{\downarrow}}
except for
i=h-1
.
Hence:

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{1}\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k+2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\downarrow}})\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}.

b)

\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)
,
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
is at the end of the arm:

As
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{c}}=(1,0)
and
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
, we have that
\rho_{0}=(\psi_{k-3})\psi_{k-2}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k},\rho_{h}=\operatorname{id},\rho_{d}=\psi_{k+1}\psi_{k}\dots\psi_{a}
and
m_{1}=k-2
.
Observe that
\rho_{0}
is the concatenation of
\chi_{h-1}
and
\chi_{h}
.

\displaystyle\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\chi_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\downarrow}}(\chi_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\downarrow}}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\psi_{k}\rho_{0}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle=(\psi_{k-3})\psi_{k-2}\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\psi_{k}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{d}z^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=(\psi_{k-3})\psi_{k-2}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-2}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}\underbrace{\dots\widehat{\rho}_{d}z^{\lambda}}_{=v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\,\;\vdots

\displaystyle=(\psi_{k-3})\psi_{k-2}\rho_{1}\Psi_{k-4}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-6}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+8}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{c+4}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}v_{\mathtt{T^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c-2}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{1}(\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{c}}{\downarrow}}\chi_{2}\dots\chi_{h-2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h-1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-8}}{\downarrow}})\chi_{h-1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{k-4}}{\downarrow}}\widehat{\rho}_{d}z^{\lambda}.

where
\widehat{\rho}_{d}=\psi_{k}\psi_{k-1}\dots\psi_{a}
.

Details for the rest of the cases can be included by switching the toggle in the
arXiv
version of this paper.

Lemma 3.25

(cf.
[
Spe15
]
, Lemma 3.23)
.

Let
\mathtt{T}\in\operatorname{Std}(\lambda)
with
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
.

a)

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(1,0)
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have reverse normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}(\rho_{d})z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a(-1)
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
y_{1}\leq k-2
and
m_{1}\geq k
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h
either
y_{i}\leq k-4i
or
y_{i}=k-4i+2
and
\rho_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
.

b)

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
is not at the end of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have reverse normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\rho_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}(\rho_{d})z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a(-1)
. Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
y_{1}\leq k-4
and
m_{1}\geq k
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h
either
y_{i}\leq k-4i-2
or
y_{i}=k-4i
and
\rho_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
.

c)

If
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
and
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
is at the end of
\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
, let
v_{\mathtt{T}}
have reverse normal form

v_{\mathtt{T}}=\psi^{*}\rho_{0}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{0}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{0}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{1}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{1}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{1}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{2}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{2}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{2}}}{\uparrow}}\dots\rho_{h}\Psi\hskip-4.0pt\underset{\scriptscriptstyle{y_{h}}}{\overset{\scriptscriptstyle{m_{h}}}{\uparrow}}\rho_{d}z^{\lambda},

where
m_{h}=a-1
.
Suppose
3\leq k\leq n-1
is odd and that
y_{1}
is minimal such that
y_{1}\leq k-4
and
m_{1}=k-2
.
Then
\psi_{k}v_{\mathtt{T}}=0
if and only if for all
i=1,2,\dots,h
either
y_{i}\leq k-4i-2
or
y_{i}=k-4i
and
\rho_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
.

Remark
.

In
3.18
, when
a
is even, we had to consider some additional restrictions on
\ell
.
However, the above lemma already states that
\mathtt{p}_{k},\mathtt{p}_{k+2}\in\operatorname{Arm}(\mathtt{T})
, hence if
\mathtt{p}_{k+2}
is at the end of the arm, this already forces
\ell=k+2
.

Proof.

After modifying the appropriate indices and notations, the proof is almost identical to the proof of
3.18
.
∎

Remark
.

Note that
3.17
part 2 corresponds to the case where
t=c
and for all
1\leq i<t
we have that
y_{i}=k-4i+2
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
(or
y_{i}=k-4i
and
\chi_{i}\neq\operatorname{id}
if
\text{\boldmath$i$}_{\mathtt{p}_{k}}=(0,1)
).
Thus,

also shows the converse of
3.17

as given in the statement then

.
